- **working-effectively-with-legacy-code**: Distilled software development rules and principles from the book 'Working Effectively With Legacy Code'. ([GeyserVelasquez/Tauros-Frontend](https://github.com/GeyserVelasquez/Tauros-Frontend/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/working-effectively-with-legacy-code))
- **working-efficiently**: How to spend tokens, models, reasoning effort and parallelism without losing quality—which work deserves the strongest model and high effort and which can go to a small, fast one; when a subagent or a parallel session pays off and when it is waste; briefing templates; context and tool-output hygiene; escalation rules and quality gates. Covers Claude Code and Codex. Use when you choose a model or effort level, before you launch a subagent or parallel session, when a session grows long or its output floods the context, and when a cheaper attempt fails. ([fireflysoftwaresolutions/devkit](https://github.com/fireflysoftwaresolutions/devkit/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/working-efficiently))
- **working-files**: Decide where a working file goes before saving it, and clean up after the job. Throwaway files go to the session scratchpad, and files a restart must not lose go to a job folder inside the project, under .claude/workforce/jobs, with a JOB.md that lets the work resume. Use before writing any file that is not the deliverable itself (notes, plans, intermediate results, downloads, logs, generated data), when a session is resumed or restarted mid-job, when a job is finished, and when the session start lists job folders left by other sessions. ([odysseyalive/claude-workforce](https://github.com/odysseyalive/claude-workforce/tree/HEAD/workforce/skills/working-files) · ★2)
